● Not yet, I was interrupted before writing it. Doing that now.

● Updated plan
  ⎿  /plan to preview

● Plan rewritten and much deeper. Here is what the second audit pass changed.

  Seven traps I found in the codebase that would each have cost Sonnet an hour 
  or more. These are now named up front with the fix, rather than left to be
  discovered:

  1. npm run verify fails on a clean tree, because Next 16 generates the ambient
  route types that layout.tsx uses only as a side effect of next build. This
  already bit the repo's CI once and the fix is in the README. Build first, then
  verify.
  2. Middleware runs on Edge, where crypto.createHmac does not exist. Session
  verification has to live in the data layer, with middleware doing a cookie
  presence check only.
  3. The Firebase service account holds only roles/datastore.user, deliberately.
  createSessionCookie() and getUserByEmail() both need more and will fail.
  verifyIdToken() without checkRevoked works fine. That is why the plan mints
  its own HMAC cookie rather than widening a documented security decision to
  make a login button work.
  4. where plus orderBy on Firestore needs a composite index that does not
  exist, and it fails at runtime rather than at build.
  5. globals.css has a global [class] { border-radius: 0 } rule with one
  carve-out.
  6. redirect() inside a Server Action works by throwing, so a try/catch
  silently kills navigation.
  7. The write rate limiter is 30 per minute per instance, so the cron sweep has
  to pace itself the way the seed script already does.

  Two corrections to my earlier draft. VoiceDictationButton already ships an
  EN/HI toggle, so WP7 syncs it rather than adding a second control. And the
  cron needs a store method that crosses owners, which the WP1 interface change
  now includes as listAllOpenCases().

  Git and deploy identity, confirmed live. gh is authed as harshkawatra11,
  Vercel CLI is logged in as the same and this directory is already linked to
  project adhikaar-oosc, and git author and committer are already Harsh Kawatra.
  New commits carry no Co-Authored-By trailer and no generated-with line. One
  honest caveat is written into the plan: four existing commits carry that
  trailer, and since history is not being rewritten, GitHub will keep listing
  Claude as a contributor until it is. The fix is a filter-repo pass and a force
  push, which I have flagged as a separate decision rather than folding it into
  this build.

  Structure now. Thirteen work packages, each with named files, the code shape
  to write, its own tests, its own acceptance check and its commit message. Plus
  a file-by-file audit table of what already exists and how to reuse it, the
  full text of the three working-folder documents, and a stuck-symptom-to-trap
  lookup at the end.
  ⎿  User rejected Claude's plan:

     │ §0 How to use this document                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ This plan is written for a model with less headroom than the one that   │

     │ First, nothing is left to inference. Where a decision could go two      │

     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ §2 Context: what exists, and why it is not finished                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Adhikaar exists and it works. Next.js 16 App Router, React 19,          │
     │ TypeScript 5, Tailwind 4,                                               │
     │ Firestore in production with a local JSON file fallback, an optional    │
     │ Gemini layer, 109 passing                                               │
     │ Vitest tests, live on Vercel. The engineering is genuinely good and the │
     │ README is genuinely                                                     │
     │ good, and several judges will read both.                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The deterministic core does four things that nothing else in this space │
     │ does:                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. It reads a citizen's problem in their own words and decides, in      │
     │ plain code, whether the                                                 │
     │ subject matter belongs to the Union or to a State under the Seventh     │
     │ Schedule of the                                                         │
     │ Constitution, then refuses to suggest a Central office for a State      │
     │ subject because DoPT                                                    │
     │ Office Memorandum No. 10/2/2008-IR means such an application is         │
     │ returned rather than                                                    │
     │ transferred, with no refund.                                            │
     │ 2. It runs eighteen legality rules over each drafted question and says  │
     │ which ones a Public                                                     │
     │ Information Officer can lawfully refuse, citing the section, with a     │
     │ mechanical rewrite where                                                │
     │ one is possible.                                                        │
     │ 3. It runs the statutory clock: thirty days under section 7(1), forty   │
     │ eight hours on the life                                                 │
     │ or liberty track, thirty five days when filed through an Assistant PIO, │
     │ thirty days to                                                          │
     │ appeal a deemed refusal under section 19(1), ninety days for a second   │
     │ appeal under section                                                    │
     │ 19(3).                                                                  │
     │ 4. It generates the actual filed document with pdf-lib: applicant       │
     │ block, correctly addressed                                              │
     │ CPIO block, the section 6(1) invocation, numbered questions, fee        │
     │ declaration, signature line.                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The problem is everything wrapped around that core.                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ It was built for an operator, not a citizen. The main workspace is at   │
     │ /docket/[id].                                                           │
     │ There is a field called "Operator notes". The intake page says "Write   │
     │ the grievance the way the                                               │
     │ citizen described it", which is instruction for a Common Service Centre │
     │ volunteer taking                                                        │
     │ dictation from somebody else. The README opens by describing it as a    │
     │ tool "for the people who                                                │
     │ file on behalf of others". The CaseStore interface has no concept of    │
     │ who owns a record, so                                                   │
     │ listCases() returns every case created by anyone and /docket shows all  │
     │ of them to whoever                                                      │
     │ opens the URL.                                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Build What Moves India judges this as a citizen. From the organiser's   │
     │ own briefing: rebuild the                                               │
     │ platform end to end with mock data, a mock backend and mock accounts;   │
     │ provide working login                                                   │
     │ credentials so the team can log in and try the product exactly like an  │
     │ end user would; only the                                                │
     │ consumer side is evaluated, the admin side is assumed; it has to open   │
     │ in a browser; and put the                                               │
     │ energy into interfaces and interactions rather than the plumbing,       │
     │ because implementation                                                  │
     │ against the real legacy stack comes later if the project is selected.   │
     │ They said twice that the                                                │
     │ citizen they have in mind is frustrated, short on time, and wants       │
     │ something faster and simpler                                            │
     │ than the site they use today.                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ So the job is not to add features to an operator tool. The job is to    │
     │ turn a proven engine into                                               │
     │ something a frustrated citizen can finish in eight minutes, sign into,  │
     │ come back to, and be                                                    │
     │ reminded by.                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ §3 The service being rebuilt, and what is actually wrong with it        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The service is rtionline.gov.in, the Department of Personnel and        │
     │ Training's RTI Online portal,                                           │
     │ version 2.0. Everything in this section was read off the portal's own   │
     │ guidelines and FAQ pages                                                │
     │ on 29 August 2026. Quotations are the portal's words, not a paraphrase. │
     │                                                                         │
     │ What a citizen actually meets there                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ You do not get an account. The FAQ says it plainly: "No. You can        │
     │ directly file your RTI on                                               │
     │ 'Submit Request' tab." Registration is optional and buys almost         │
     │ nothing. Your case is tracked                                           │
     │ by a registration number plus the email address you typed at submission │
     │ time. There is no page                                                  │
     │ anywhere that lists the applications you have filed. Lose the number    │
     │ and you have lost the                                                   │
     │ case. The portal states that it retains RTI cases for three years,      │
     │ after which they are gone.                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ You have to already know which government owns your problem. The portal │
     │ covers Central                                                          │
     │ ministries and departments only, and says so: applications may be filed │
     │ "for all                                                                │
     │ Ministries/Departments and other Public Authorities of Central          │
     │ Government", and users                                                  │
     │ "should not" file for state government authorities through it. It does  │
     │ not ask what your                                                       │
     │ problem is and then work out which government owns it. It presents a    │
     │ cascading Ministry to                                                   │
     │ Department to Public Authority dropdown and expects you to choose       │
     │ correctly. The FAQ explains                                             │
     │ what happens when you choose wrong: a Nodal Officer will transfer it    │
     │ electronically if the                                                   │
     │ correct authority is also on the portal, or physically otherwise, under │
     │ section 6(3). What the                                                  │
     │ FAQ does not say is that DoPT Office Memorandum No. 10/2/2008-IR of 12  │
     │ June 2008 directs that                                                  │
     │ where the information concerns a State public authority, a Central PIO  │
     │ need not transfer at                                                    │
     │ all. It goes back to you, and the fee does not.                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ One 3000 character textarea, and no help inside it. "At present, the    │
     │ text of the                                                             │
     │ application is confined up to 3000 characters only." Longer requests go │
     │ up as a PDF                                                             │
     │ attachment. There is no warning that a question beginning with "why" is │
     │ asking the authority to                                                 │
     │ form an opinion rather than produce a record, and can be refused under  │
     │ section 2(f) without the                                                │
     │ officer ever reaching a section 8 exemption. No warning that "all files │
     │ since inception"                                                        │
     │ invites a disproportionate diversion refusal. No warning that asking    │
     │ for a named individual's                                                │
     │ salary now falls under the amended section 8(1)(j), which since 13      │
     │ November 2025 no longer                                                 │
     │ carries a public interest override or the old proviso protecting        │
     │ information that could not be                                           │
     │ denied to Parliament.                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ After you submit, the portal goes silent. You get a registration        │
     │ number. Nothing counts the                                              │
     │ thirty days for you. Nothing tells you, on the day it lapses, that you  │
     │ now hold a deemed refusal                                               │
     │ under section 7(2), that you have thirty days to file a first appeal    │
     │ under section 19(1), and                                                │
     │ that under section 7(6) the information must now be provided free of    │
     │ charge. Miss that window                                                │
     │ and the remedy is gone. The portal does not consider this its job.      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The first appeal makes you start over. Select "Submit First Appeal",    │
     │ type in the original                                                    │
     │ registration number and email again, fill the form again.               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ What it does well, and say so. Payment is genuinely modern: net         │
     │ banking, Visa and                                                       │
     │ Mastercard debit and credit, RuPay, and UPI. The BPL exemption is       │
     │ honoured, and no fee is                                                 │
     │ required from a citizen below the poverty line who attaches the         │
     │ certificate. Email and SMS                                              │
     │ alerts exist. A help desk exists with a published number and hours. Do  │
     │ not sneer at this portal                                                │
     │ anywhere in the product or the pitch. It works, it is genuinely useful, │
     │ and the respectful                                                      │
     │ framing is both truer and more persuasive to a judge than contempt      │
     │ would be.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The scale of what the gap costs                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Figure: RTI applications transferred between public authorities,        │
     │ 2024-25                                                                 │
     │ Value: 2,80,353, against 2,37,671 the previous year, roughly 18 percent │
     │ up                                                                      │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: CIC Annual Report 2024-25. Collected from  │
     │ reporting on the report, not the report itself. Confirm in the PDF.     │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Applications rejected, 2024-25                                  │
     │ Value: approximately 58,500 by the CIC's headline count, 57,985 by its  │
     │ own                                                                     │
     │ exemption-wise table                                                    │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: CIC Annual Report 2024-25. The two figures │
     │                                                                         │
     │ disagreeing is itself worth one line in the pitch.                      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Public authorities absent from the CIC's 2024-25 report despite │
     │ a claim                                                                 │
     │ of 100 percent compliance                                               │
     │ Value: 611                                                              │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: CIC Annual Report 2024-25, as analysed by  │
     │ Counterview, June 2026                                                  │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Appeals and complaints pending across 29 Information            │
     │ Commissions at 30                                                       │
     │ June 2025                                                               │
     │ Value: more than four lakh                                              │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: Satark Nagrik Sangathan, Report Card of    │
     │ Information Commissions 2024-25                                         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Worst state backlogs                                            │
     │ Value: Maharashtra 95,340, Karnataka 47,825, Tamil Nadu 41,059          │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: same                                       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Telangana's backlog at its present disposal rate                │
     │ Value: approximately 29 years                                           │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: same                                       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Cases where a penalty could have been imposed on a PIO and was  │
     │ not                                                                     │
     │ Value: approximately 98 percent                                         │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: same                                       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Commissions that had not published a 2023-24 annual report      │
     │ Value: 20 of 29                                                         │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: same                                       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Figure: Commissions non-functional for some period due to unfilled      │
     │ commissioner                                                            │
     │ posts                                                                   │
     │ Value: 6: Jharkhand, Himachal Pradesh, Telangana, Goa, Tripura, Madhya  │
     │ Pradesh                                                                 │
     │ Source, and what to confirm: same                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Alongside those, the primary evidence already parsed and living in      │
     │ src/lib/data/evidence.ts, which is stronger than any of the above       │
     │ because it is one                                                       │
     │ department's own published register rather than a national aggregate:   │
     │ the Department of Land                                                  │
     │ Resources RTI disposal register, 3,128 uniquely numbered applications,  │
     │ 2,797 returned to the                                                   │
     │ applicant unanswered, 265 disposed of, 148 transferred. Source PDF URL  │
     │ and the honesty caveat                                                  │
     │ are already in that file. The caveat matters and must travel with the   │
     │ number every time it is                                                 │
     │ shown: land is a State subject, so this department's return rate is far │
     │ above the national norm                                                 │
     │ and must never be quoted as a national figure.                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Confirmation duty, and it is not optional. Before any figure from the   │
     │ table above renders                                                     │
     │ in the app, open the source and confirm it yourself:                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - https://cic.gov.in/sites/default/files/Reports/AR2024-25E.pdf. The    │
     │ file is large and a                                                     │
     │ plain fetch may fail on size. Download it with curl -L -o into the      │
     │ scratch directory and                                                   │
     │ read it locally.                                                        │
     │ - https://www.snsindia.org/rti-assessments/                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Record what you confirmed, with the date, in                            │
     │ build-what-india-moves/RESEARCH.md. Any figure                          │
     │ that does not confirm gets dropped or marked unverified in the          │
     │ interface. Do not carry a                                               │
     │ number into the pitch on the strength of this plan alone. This plan is  │
     │ a research summary, not                                                 │
     │ a primary source, and it says so about itself.                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The incumbents in the neighbouring features, named                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Name these in the product. Pretending they do not exist is the easiest  │
     │ way to lose a technical                                                 │
     │ judge, and naming them is the cheapest way to look like you did the     │
     │ homework.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - myscheme.gov.in, the government's own scheme discovery portal, is the │
     │ incumbent for the                                                       │
     │ Scheme Eligibility Reader. What is different here: this one shows the   │
     │ rule it applied and                                                     │
     │ hands you a way to challenge the answer in writing.                     │
     │ - RTI drafting services such as the various paid "file my RTI"          │
     │ operators draft the                                                     │
     │ application. None of them decide whether the authority you picked can   │
     │ lawfully answer it.                                                     │
     │ - General legal chatbots answer rights questions without a source. The  │
     │ Rights Navigator                                                        │
     │ declines to answer when it has no source, which is the difference.      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ §4 Codebase audit: what exists, and what you reuse                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Read this table before touching anything. The right column is the       │
     │ single most useful thing in                                             │
     │ this document, because most of the work in this build is wiring         │
     │ existing correct code into a                                            │
     │ new shell rather than writing new logic.                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The deterministic core, all of which you keep                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ File: src/lib/types.ts                                                  │
     │ What it does: CaseRecord, Applicant, JurisdictionTriageResult,          │
     │ RemedyTriageResult, DraftQuestion, LintFinding, Deadline, FeeInfo,      │
     │ CorpusChunk                                                             │
     │ How you use it: You add ownerUid in WP1 and a few optional fields       │
     │ later.                                                                  │
     │ Nothing else changes.                                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/jurisdiction.ts                                           │
     │ What it does: runJurisdictionTriage({ grievanceText, state?,            │
     │ selectedAuthorityId? }) returns level, subject matter, Seventh Schedule │
     │                                                                         │
     │ entry, up to 3 authority candidates with confidence and reasoning, and  │
     │ a                                                                       │
     │ blockingWarning when a State subject meets a Central authority          │
     │ How you use it: Called by the interview in WP4. Do not modify.          │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/remedy.ts                                                 │
     │ What it does: runRemedyTriage(text) classifies into rti, consumer,      │
     │ labour,                                                                 │
     │ tenancy, grievance or hybrid, with forum name, limitation period, and   │
     │ consumer pecuniary jurisdiction computed on price paid against the 2021 │
     │ Rules                                                                   │
     │ How you use it: Called by the interview in WP4 and is what lets the     │
     │ product                                                                 │
     │ refuse to file an RTI when an RTI is the wrong instrument. Do not       │
     │ modify.                                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/linter/rules.ts                                           │
     │ What it does: lintQuestion(text) returns findings from 18 rules, each   │
     │ with                                                                    │
     │ severity block/warn/info, a plain language explanation, a citation id,  │
     │ and                                                                     │
     │ sometimes a mechanical rewrite                                          │
     │ How you use it: Runs live in the composer. Do not modify.               │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/deadlines.ts                                              │
     │ What it does: computeInitialDeadlines,                                  │
     │ computeDeemedRefusalAppealWindow,                                       │
     │ computeFirstAppealDisposalDeadline, computeSecondAppealWindow,          │
     │ isOverdue,                                                              │
     │ daysUntil                                                               │
     │ How you use it: Powers the countdown card in WP3 and the cron in WP8.   │
     │ Do not                                                                  │
     │ modify.                                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/sweep.ts                                                  │
     │ What it does: sweepCase(record, now) transitions awaiting_response to   │
     │ first_appeal_drafted on a lapsed deadline and produces a full first     │
     │ appeal                                                                  │
     │ text citing sections 7(2), 19(1) and 7(6)                               │
     │ How you use it: The cron in WP8 calls this. The appeal text it produces │
     │ is the                                                                  │
     │ moneyshot. Do not modify.                                               │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/data/seventh-schedule.ts                                  │
     │ What it does: 16 subject mappings to List I, II or III with entry       │
     │ numbers, plus                                                           │
     │ classifySubjectMatter(text)                                             │
     │ How you use it: Do not modify. If you widen it, the npm run eval gold   │
     │ set                                                                     │
     │ numbers move and you have broken the published evaluation.              │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/data/authorities.ts                                       │
     │ What it does: 15 authorities: 12 central, Delhi and Maharashtra at      │
     │ department                                                              │
     │ level. Each with CPIO address, FAA address, subject keywords and a      │
     │ coverage                                                                │
     │ note. Plus DIRECTORY_COVERAGE_STATEMENT                                 │
     │ How you use it: Read from directly. The coverage statement is written   │
     │ for                                                                     │
     │ exactly the "we do not cover your state" screen you need in WP4.        │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/data/state-fees.ts                                        │
     │ What it does: CENTRAL_FEE, STATE_FEES for 9 states with the confidence  │
     │ flag,                                                                   │
     │ BPL_EXEMPTION_NOTE, lookupStateFee(state)                               │
     │ How you use it: Read from directly. Study the confidence pattern, you   │
     │ will                                                                    │
     │ reuse it for schemes in WP6.                                            │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/data/rti-act.ts                                           │
     │ What it does: RTI_ACT_CORPUS, an array of CorpusChunk with verbatim     │
     │ statutory                                                               │
     │ text, plus getCorpusChunk(id)                                           │

     │ Read it                                                                 │
     │ before writing a single new chunk.                                      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/data/evidence.ts                                          │
     │ What it does: DOLR_EVIDENCE, NATIONAL_CONTEXT, CSC_CONTEXT, all with    │
     │ source                                                                  │
     │ URLs and caveats                                                        │
     │ How you use it: Landing page evidence, WP10.                            │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/citations.ts                                              │
     │ What it does: resolveCitation throws UnresolvedCitationError,           │
     │ tryResolveCitation returns null. The single choke point that stops an   │
     │ unsourced legal claim rendering                                         │
     │ How you use it: WP5 repoints its import at a merged corpus. Nothing     │
     │ else                                                                    │
     │ changes.                                                                │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/pdf/generate.ts                                           │

     │ and                                                                     │
     │ Times Roman                                                             │
     │ How you use it: Called by the existing PDF route. WP4 feeds it, WP12    │
     │ checks the                                                              │
     │ output opens.                                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The persistence layer                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ File: src/lib/store.ts                                                  │

     │ listCases/getCase/createCase/updateCase/deleteCase to Firestore when    │
     │ the                                                                     │
     │ three FIREBASE_* vars are set, otherwise to the file store              │
     │ Change needed: WP1 adds ownerUid to every signature and adds one        │
     │ admin-only                                                              │
     │ function for the cron                                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/store/types.ts                                            │
     │ What it does: The CaseStore interface both backends implement           │
     │ Change needed: WP1 rewrites the interface                               │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/store/firestoreStore.ts                                   │
     │ What it does: Admin SDK, collection cases, assertWriteAllowed() before  │
     │ every                                                                   │
     │ write                                                                   │
     │ Change needed: WP1 adds the owner filter. Read the note in §5 about the │
     │                                                                         │
     │ composite index before you touch listCases                              │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/store/fileStore.ts                                        │
     │ What it does: JSON at .data/cases.json locally,                         │
     │ os.tmpdir()/adhikaar-data on                                            │

     │ Change needed: WP1 mirrors the owner logic                              │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/firestore/client.ts                                       │
     │ What it does: Admin app singleton. Note ignoreUndefinedProperties: true │
     │ is                                                                      │
     │ already set, so optional fields on CaseRecord are safe to add. Note the │
     │ \\n                                                                     │
     │ to newline fix on the private key                                       │
     │ Change needed: No change                                                │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/firestore/rateLimit.ts                                    │
     │ What it does: 30 writes per minute per instance, in-process fixed       │
     │ window,                                                                 │
     │ throws RateLimitExceededError                                           │
     │ Change needed: No change, but any bulk write you add must pace itself.  │
     │ The seed                                                                │
     │ script already does                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The Gemini layer, which is optional by design                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ File: src/lib/gemini/client.ts                                          │
     │ What it does: isGeminiConfigured(), getGeminiModelChain(),              │

     │ Tries                                                                   │
     │ GEMINI_MODEL if set, then gemini-3.7-flash, gemini-3.6-flash,           │
     │ gemini-2.5-flash, moving on after any request failure or empty          │
     │ response.                                                               │
     │ Throws typed GeminiNotConfiguredError / GeminiAllModelsFailedError      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │

     │ What it does: generatePlainLanguageCopy(record). Its system instruction │

     │ prohibitions, output-only-the-text                                      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ File: src/lib/gemini/rewritePolish.ts                                   │
     │ What it does: polishRewrite(original, mechanicalRewrite). Given the     │
     │ meaning it                                                              │
     │ must preserve. Never replaces the deterministic rewrite, only sits      │
     │ beside it                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Every new Gemini call in this build goes through generateWithFallback.  │
     │ Never call                                                              │
     │ ai.models.generateContent directly. That is how the fallback chain      │
     │ stays worth having.                                                     │

     │ src/app/globals.css holds a complete token layer: parchment --paper,    │

     │ acts. Fonts are Fraunces for display, Source Serif 4 for body, IBM Plex │

     │ distinctive, it                                                         │

     │ other several thousand                                                  │
     │ submissions. Extend tokens if you need to. Replace nothing.             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ §5 Architecture decisions, and the traps                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ These are the things that will cost you an hour each if you meet them   │
     │ by surprise. Read all of                                                │
     │ them now.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 1: npm run verify fails on a clean checkout, and it is not your    │
     │ fault                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ package.json defines verify as lint && typecheck && test && build. On a │
     │ checkout that has                                                       │
     │ never been built, typecheck fails, because Next.js 16 generates ambient │
     │ route types such as                                                     │
     │ LayoutProps<"/"> (used in src/app/layout.tsx) as a side effect of next  │
     │ build or                                                                │
     │ next dev. A fresh tree has no .next/types and tsc --noEmit cannot find  │
     │ them.                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ This exact thing already happened once in this repo's CI and the fix is │
     │ recorded in the README:                                                 │
     │ the standalone typecheck step was removed from .github/workflows/ci.yml │
     │ because next build                                                      │
     │ performs the same check with the generated types present.               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ So: run npm run build once before you ever run npm run verify. If       │
     │ typecheck fails                                                         │
     │ with a missing LayoutProps or PageProps type, that is this, not a real  │
     │ error. Do not                                                           │
     │ "fix" it by loosening tsconfig.json and do not add the typecheck step   │
     │ back into CI.                                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 2: Do not verify the session signature in middleware               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Next.js middleware runs on the Edge runtime by default. Node's          │
     │ crypto.createHmac does not                                              │
     │ exist there. If you write signature verification into middleware.ts     │
     │ using node crypto, the                                                  │
     │ build fails or the middleware throws at runtime, and the error message  │
     │ will not point at the                                                   │
     │ runtime as the cause.                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The pattern, which is also what Next's own auth guidance recommends:    │
     │ middleware performs an                                                  │
     │ optimistic check only, meaning does the session cookie exist, and       │
     │ redirects to /login if not.                                             │
     │ Authoritative verification happens in getSession() in                   │
     │ src/lib/auth/session.ts, which runs in                                  │
     │ Server Components, Server Actions and Route Handlers where the Node     │
     │ runtime is guaranteed and                                               │
     │ crypto.createHmac works normally. Every protected page and every Server │
     │ Action calls                                                            │
     │ requireSession(). Middleware is a redirect convenience, not the         │
     │ security boundary.                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 3: Do not use createSessionCookie() or getUserByEmail()            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The Firebase service account behind this project holds exactly one IAM  │
     │ role,                                                                   │
     │ roles/datastore.user, on a dedicated GCP project. This is deliberate    │
     │ and the README describes                                                │
     │ it as a feature: the account cannot touch billing, cannot create        │
     │ resources, cannot read outside                                          │
     │ this Firestore database.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ admin.auth().createSessionCookie() needs iam.serviceAccounts.signBlob   │
     │ on top of that.                                                         │
     │ admin.auth().getUserByEmail() and listUsers() hit the Identity Toolkit  │
     │ API and need a                                                          │
     │ Firebase Auth admin role. Calling either will fail with a permission    │
     │ error, and the fix people                                               │
     │ reach for is widening the service account, which would undo a           │
     │ documented security decision to                                         │
     │ make a login button work.                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ admin.auth().verifyIdToken(token) does work with the current            │
     │ permissions, because it                                                 │
     │ verifies a JWT against Google's public signing certificates and does    │
     │ not call a project-scoped                                               │
     │ admin API. Do not pass checkRevoked: true, which does call Identity     │
     │ Toolkit.                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ So: verify the ID token, then mint our own HMAC signed cookie. Details  │
     │ in WP2.                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ For the demo account's uid, which the seed script needs, do not call    │
     │ getUserByEmail. Create                                                  │
     │ the account once in the Firebase console, copy the uid from the Users   │
     │ tab, and put it in a                                                    │
     │ DEMO_UID environment variable. One manual step, zero permission         │
     │ changes.                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 4: Firestore where plus orderBy needs a composite index            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ db.collection("cases").where("ownerUid","==",uid).orderBy("updatedAt"," │
     │ desc") requires a                                                       │
     │ composite index that does not exist. Firestore fails this at runtime    │
     │ with an error containing a                                              │
     │ link to create the index, which is exactly the kind of failure that     │
     │ appears thirty seconds                                                  │
     │ before a judge opens the app.                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Query with where only, then sort in JavaScript. Cases per user are      │
     │ small. This is correct                                                  │
     │ here, not a shortcut.                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 5: the global square corner rule                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ src/app/globals.css contains [class] { border-radius: 0; }. Every       │
     │ element that has any                                                    │
     │ class attribute gets square corners. This is intentional and is a       │
     │ signature of the design. If                                             │
     │ you need something round, give it the .seal-mark class, which is the    │
     │ one exception already                                                   │
     │ carved out. Do not add !important overrides, and do not remove the      │
     │ rule. Somebody already                                                  │
     │ scoped this correctly once and removed an !important, per the handoff   │
     │ doc.                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 6: redirect() inside a Server Action                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ intakeAction in src/lib/actions.ts calls redirect() from                │
     │ next/navigation. That works by                                          │
     │ throwing a control-flow error Next catches. If you wrap a Server Action │
     │ body in try/catch                                                       │
     │ around a redirect(), you swallow it and the navigation silently does    │
     │ not happen. Put the                                                     │
     │ redirect outside the try, or re-throw anything matching Next's redirect │
     │ error.                                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Trap 7: the write rate limiter                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ assertWriteAllowed() allows 30 writes per minute per server instance.   │
     │ The seed script already                                                 │
     │ paces itself against this, and somebody had to fix it once to make it   │
     │ do so. Any new bulk write                                               │
     │ path you add, including anything in the cron sweep that touches many    │
     │ cases, must pace itself the                                             │
     │ same way or it will throw RateLimitExceededError partway through and    │
     │ leave data half written.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decision: session mechanism                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Firebase Auth for identity, our own cookie for the session.             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Client signs in with the Firebase web SDK. Three paths: one-click    │
     │ demo, Google popup, email                                               │
     │ and password.                                                           │
     │ 2. Client sends the resulting ID token to POST /api/auth/session.       │
     │ 3. That route calls verifyIdToken, then builds a payload { uid, email,  │
     │ name, exp }, signs it                                                   │
     │ with HMAC SHA-256 using SESSION_SECRET and node crypto, and sets it as  │
     │ an httpOnly,                                                            │
     │ secure, sameSite: "lax" cookie named adhikaar_session with a fourteen   │
     │ day expiry.                                                             │
     │ 4. getSession() reads and verifies. requireSession() redirects to       │
     │ /login when there is no                                                 │
     │ valid session.                                                          │
     │ 5. DELETE /api/auth/session clears the cookie. The client also calls    │
     │ Firebase signOut().                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Firebase client config values go in NEXT_PUBLIC_* variables. That is    │
     │ correct and not a leak:                                                 │
     │ Firebase web API keys are public identifiers, not secrets. The security │
     │ boundary is the admin                                                   │
     │ SDK server-side plus Firestore rules.                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Firestore rules stay locked. The browser never talks to Firestore       │
     │ directly in this app;                                                   │
     │ every read and write goes through the admin SDK on the server. Set the  │
     │ rules to deny all client                                                │
     │ access and leave them that way. Do not add client-side Firestore reads. │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decision: Rights Navigator retrieval, no vector database                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The failure everybody else ships is a chatbot with government PDFs      │
     │ stuffed into it, answering                                              │
     │ confidently and wrongly. The research document in hacker-villa names    │
     │ this anti-pattern                                                       │
     │ explicitly. Do not build it.                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ At this corpus size, roughly forty to sixty chunks, a plain lexical     │
     │ scorer beats an embedding                                               │
     │ pipeline on accuracy, on honesty, and on being a pure function you can  │
     │ unit test. It needs no                                                  │
     │ external service, no API key, no index build step, and it cannot        │
     │ silently drift. Full spec in                                            │
     │ WP5.                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The one non-negotiable behaviour: a retrieval floor. Below it the       │
     │ Navigator says it has no                                                │
     │ source and offers what it can do instead. It does not answer. A judge   │
     │ will type "how do I get a                                               │
     │ divorce" and that decline is the moment that separates this from the    │
     │ crowd.                                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decision: the interview is a deterministic graph, not an agent          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Every step of the intake interview is a node with an id, prompts in     │
     │ both languages, an input                                                │
     │ type, and a pure function choosing the next node from the answers so    │
     │ far. Testable, cheap,                                                   │
     │ and it cannot invent a step in front of a judge. An LLM-driven          │
     │ interview will go off the rails                                         │
     │ at the worst possible moment and there is no upside that justifies the  │
     │ risk.                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Decision: what stays out of scope, stated in the interface              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - No submission to any government portal. There is no public API and    │
     │ pretending otherwise                                                    │
     │ would be dishonest. Adhikaar prepares the document and tells you        │
     │ exactly where to take it.                                               │
     │ - The Rights Navigator is not legal advice, says so on every answer,    │
     │ and never outputs a                                                     │
     │ directive like "you should sue".                                        │
     │ - No claim of comprehensive statutory, authority or scheme coverage     │
     │ anywhere. The product names                                             │
     │ what it covers: twelve central departments, two states, a bounded       │
     │ statute corpus, eight                                                   │
     │ schemes.                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ §6 Work packages                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Thirteen packages. Each ends green and gets one commit. The commit      │
     │ message for each is given.                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ WP0: Set up the working folder and confirm the research                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ No code. Do this first because everything downstream quotes from it.    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Create build-what-india-moves/RESEARCH.md from the template in §7.1. │
     │ Then go and confirm                                                     │
     │ each figure in the §3 table against its source, and fill in the         │
     │ confirmation column with the                                            │
     │ date and what you actually saw. Download the CIC report with            │
     │ curl -L -o rather than fetching it, it is large.                        │
     │ 2. Create build-what-india-moves/BUILD-LOG.md from the template in      │
     │ §7.2.                                                                   │
     │ 3. Create build-what-india-moves/SUBMISSION.md from the template in     │
     │ §7.3, leaving the link                                                  │
     │ and credentials blank for now.                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Check: every figure in RESEARCH.md is either confirmed with a date or   │
     │ struck through with                                                     │
     │ a note saying it did not confirm. No figure sits there unmarked.        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Commit: not a code commit. These files live outside the repo.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ WP1: Ownership in the data model                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Every case is currently visible to everyone. Fix this first, because    │
     │ every later package                                                     │
     │ depends on knowing who owns a record.                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Files: src/lib/types.ts, src/lib/store/types.ts, src/lib/store.ts,      │
     │ src/lib/store/fileStore.ts, src/lib/store/firestoreStore.ts,            │
     │ src/lib/actions.ts,                                                     │
     │ src/app/api/cases/[id]/pdf/route.ts                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Steps:                                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. In src/lib/types.ts, add to CaseRecord directly under status:        │
     │ /** Firebase Auth uid of the citizen who owns this filing. Every store  │
     │  *  method takes this and compares it, so a caller that forgets cannot  │
     │  *  leak another citizen's grievance text. */                           │
     │ ownerUid: string;                                                       │
     │ 2. Rewrite src/lib/store/types.ts:                                      │
     │ import type { CaseRecord } from "@/lib/types";                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ /** Every backing store, file or Firestore, implements exactly this.    │
     │  *  Ownership is enforced inside the store rather than by the caller,   │
     │  *  because there are many callers and one of them will forget. */      │
     │ export interface CaseStore {                                            │
     │   listCases(ownerUid: string): Promise<CaseRecord[]>;                   │
     │   getCase(id: string, ownerUid: string): Promise<CaseRecord |           │
     │ undefined>;                                                             │
     │   createCase(                                                           │
     │     partial: Omit<CaseRecord, "id" | "createdAt" | "updatedAt">         │
     │   ): Promise<CaseRecord>;                                               │
     │   updateCase(                                                           │
     │     id: string,                                                         │
     │     ownerUid: string,                                                   │
     │     patch: Partial<Omit<CaseRecord, "id" | "createdAt" | "ownerUid">>   │
     │   ): Promise<CaseRecord | undefined>;                                   │
     │   deleteCase(id: string, ownerUid: string): Promise<boolean>;           │
     │   /** Cron only. Returns every case with an open deadline across all    │
     │    *  owners. No route reachable by a browser may call this. */         │
     │   listAllOpenCases(): Promise<CaseRecord[]>;                            │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │ 2. Note ownerUid is excluded from the update patch type. A case cannot  │
     │ change hands.                                                           │
     │ 3. In firestoreStore.ts:                                                │
     │   - listCases(ownerUid): where("ownerUid", "==", ownerUid).get(), then  │
     │ .sort((a, b) => b.updatedAt.localeCompare(a.updatedAt)) in JavaScript.  │
     │ Do not chain                                                            │
     │ .orderBy(). See Trap 4.                                                 │
     │   - getCase(id, ownerUid): read the doc, and if it does not exist or    │
     │ data.ownerUid !== ownerUid, return undefined. Not an error. A wrong     │
     │ owner must be                                                           │
     │ indistinguishable from a missing case.                                  │
     │   - updateCase(id, ownerUid, patch): same ownership check, return       │
     │ undefined on mismatch.                                                  │
     │   - deleteCase(id, ownerUid): same, return false on mismatch.           │
     │   - listAllOpenCases(): where("status", "in", ["awaiting_response",     │
     │ "first_appeal_filed"]).                                                 │
     │ A single-field in filter needs no composite index.                      │
     │ 4. Mirror all of it in fileStore.ts.                                    │
     │ 5. In src/lib/store.ts, update every exported wrapper to take and pass  │
     │ ownerUid, and export                                                    │
     │ listAllOpenCases.                                                       │
     │ 6. In src/lib/actions.ts, every action now needs a uid. WP2 supplies it │
     │ from the session. For                                                   │
     │ now add at the top of the file:                                         │
     │ // TODO(WP2): replaced by requireSession() once auth lands.             │
     │ const TEMP_OWNER_UID = "seed-owner";                                    │
     │ 6. and thread it through. Delete this in WP2.                           │
     │ 7. src/app/api/cases/[id]/pdf/route.ts calls getCase(id). Give it the   │
     │ temp uid too, marked                                                    │
     │ with the same TODO.                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New test: src/lib/store/ownership.test.ts, run against fileStore with a │
     │ temporary data                                                          │
     │ directory. Four assertions minimum:                                     │
     │ - a case created by uid A is not returned by getCase(id, "B")           │
     │ - it does not appear in listCases("B")                                  │
     │ - updateCase(id, "B", patch) returns undefined and leaves the record    │
     │ unchanged                                                               │
     │ - deleteCase(id, "B") returns false and the record still exists         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Check: npm run build first (Trap 1), then npm run test. All existing    │
     │ tests plus the new                                                      │
     │ ones pass.                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Commit: Scope every case to an owner, enforced inside the store         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ WP2: Accounts, sessions, and the demo door                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New files:                                                              │
     │ src/lib/auth/firebaseClient.ts                                          │
     │ src/lib/auth/session.ts                                                 │
     │ src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts                                       │
     │ src/app/login/page.tsx                                                  │
     │ src/components/auth/LoginForm.tsx                                       │
     │ src/components/auth/SignOutButton.tsx                                   │
     │ middleware.ts                      (repo root, beside package.json)     │
     │ Edited: src/lib/actions.ts, src/app/api/cases/[id]/pdf/route.ts,        │
     │ src/components/SiteHeader.tsx, .env.example                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Environment variables. Add to .env.example and to Vercel in all three   │
     │ environments:                                                           │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY=                                           │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN=                                       │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=                                        │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_APP_ID=                                            │
     │ SESSION_SECRET=                                                         │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_EMAIL=demo@adhikaar.in                                 │
     │ NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_PASSWORD=                                              │
     │ DEMO_UID=                                                               │
     │ The demo password is deliberately public. It is printed on the login    │
     │ page for judges anyway.                                                 │
     │ SESSION_SECRET is a real secret, generate it with openssl rand -hex 32. │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Manual step before coding: in the Firebase console for this project,    │
     │ enable the Email and                                                    │
     │ Password provider and the Google provider, create the demo@adhikaar.in  │
     │ account with the                                                        │
     │ password you chose, and copy its uid into DEMO_UID. This is the         │
     │ one-time step that avoids                                               │
     │ needing getUserByEmail (Trap 3).                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ src/lib/auth/session.ts, the shape to write:                            │
     │ import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from "crypto";                   │
     │ import { cookies } from "next/headers";                                 │
     │ import { redirect } from "next/navigation";                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ const COOKIE = "adhikaar_session";                                      │
     │ const MAX_AGE_SECONDS = 60 * 60 * 24 * 14;                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ export interface Session {                                              │
     │   uid: string;                                                          │
     │   email: string;                                                        │
     │   name: string;                                                         │
     │   exp: number;                                                          │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ function b64url(input: string): string { /* Buffer base64 then + - / _  │
     │ = stripped */ }                                                         │
     │ function sign(payload: string): string { /* createHmac("sha256",        │
     │ SESSION_SECRET) */ }                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ export function encodeSession(session: Session): string { /* payload +  │
     │ "." + sig */ }                                                          │
     │ export function decodeSession(raw: string): Session | null {            │
     │   // split on ".", recompute the signature, compare with                │
     │ timingSafeEqual                                                         │
     │   // on equal-length buffers, then check exp against Date.now()/1000.   │
     │   // Return null on any failure. Never throw here.                      │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ export async function getSession(): Promise<Session | null> { /* read   │
     │ cookie, decode */ }                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ export async function requireSession(): Promise<Session> {              │
     │   const s = await getSession();                                         │
     │   if (!s) redirect("/login");                                           │
     │   return s;                                                             │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │ timingSafeEqual throws when the two buffers differ in length, so        │
     │ compare lengths first and                                               │
     │ return null rather than letting it throw.                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ src/app/api/auth/session/route.ts:                                      │
     │ - POST: read { idToken } from the body, verifyIdToken(idToken) with the │
     │ admin SDK (no                                                           │
     │ checkRevoked), build the session from the decoded token's uid, email    │
     │ and name, set the                                                       │
     │ cookie with httpOnly: true, secure: process.env.NODE_ENV ===            │
     │ "production",                                                           │
     │ sameSite: "lax", path: "/", maxAge: MAX_AGE_SECONDS. Return { ok: true  │
     │ }.                                                                      │
     │ - DELETE: delete the cookie, return { ok: true }.                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ middleware.ts, presence check only (Trap 2):                            │
     │ import { NextResponse, type NextRequest } from "next/server";           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ export function middleware(req: NextRequest) {                          │
     │   const hasCookie = Boolean(req.cookies.get("adhikaar_session"));       │
     │   if (!hasCookie) {                                                     │
     │     const url = req.nextUrl.clone();                                    │
     │     url.pathname = "/login";                                            │
     │     url.searchParams.set("next", req.nextUrl.pathname);                 │
     │     return NextResponse.redirect(url);                                  │
     │   }                                                                     │
     │   return NextResponse.next();                                           │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ // Optimistic only. Real verification is getSession() in the data       │
     │ layer,                                                                  │
     │ // because the Edge runtime has no node crypto and this must not become │
     │ // the security boundary.                                               │
     │ export const config = { matcher: ["/my/:path*", "/start/:path*"] };     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ src/app/login/page.tsx. This screen matters more than it sounds. A      │
     │ judge with two hundred                                                  │
     │ tabs open will bounce off a login wall. Order on the page, top to       │
     │ bottom:                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. One large primary button, "Enter as a demo citizen". One click, no   │
     │ typing. It calls                                                        │
     │ signInWithEmailAndPassword with the two NEXT_PUBLIC_DEMO_* values.      │
     │ 2. Immediately below, a bordered panel with the credentials as plain    │
     │ selectable text:                                                        │
     │ demo@adhikaar.in and the password. Some judges will want to type them   │
     │ to prove it is real.                                                    │
     │ Let them.                                                               │
     │ 3. Google sign in.                                                      │
     │ 4. Email and password form, with a link to create an account.           │
     │ 5. One line on what happens to their data, because this app holds       │
     │ grievance text that can                                                 │
     │ contain personal information.                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Every path ends the same way: get the ID token, POST /api/auth/session, │
     │ then                                                                    │
     │ router.push(next ?? "/my") and router.refresh(). The refresh matters,   │
     │ without it the server                                                   │
     │ components will not see the new cookie.                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Handle the Google popup being blocked with a visible message rather     │
     │ than a silent failure.                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Wire the session in. Delete TEMP_OWNER_UID. Every Server Action in      │
     │ src/lib/actions.ts                                                      │
     │ starts with const { uid } = await requireSession(); and passes uid into │
     │ the store. The PDF                                                      │
     │ route does the same. intakeAction sets ownerUid: uid on the created     │
